<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.

If explicit user input is required for another reason before progress can safely continue, do not use the `request_user_input` tool. Ask the user directly with one concise plain-text question instead. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.
</collaboration_mode>

<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user\'s behalf. No global-memory edits, publication, messages, or external-system changes. Do not request or save credentials; use references or redactions for secrets.\n- The starter kit ignores `brainstorms/` in Git by default. This does not remove captures already tracked in an existing repository.\n- Validate a fresh session, a same-day name collision, a resume, a correcting answer, a stop request, and a failed write. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.\n\nWork the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven't heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.\n\nFormat a round like so:\n\n```\n❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n```\n\nEach round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.\n\nFinding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it; don't ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don't block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent to report; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.\n\nThe session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.\n\r\n"}]

Small mono beside geometric sans is what produces the editorial, indexed, documented feel — and it fixes the hierarchy inversion for free, because meta stops competing with headings.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:309:4. **Lecture video** — conditional; largest object on the page when present (slot only in this build)\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:310:5. **Lecture notes** — conditional, images and GIFs inline\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:311:6. **Drills done** — conditional\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:312:7. **Up next** — conditional\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:313:8. **This week** — conditional\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:314:9. **Bootcamp** — conditional\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:315:10. **Wrong-answer queue** — conditional on having wrong answers\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md:316:11. **Uploads** — submit control always present; parse rows conditional\r\nprojects/lsatu\\classroom-mockup.html:268:  [\'Conditional logic: the contrapositive drill\',\'17 Jul 2026\'],\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-ROUTING-AUDIT.md:167:**Confirmed live.** `/analytics` renders a header reading **"Workspace"** because the map has no `/analytics` entry; `/sets` is also missing; `/admin/board` is mapped but never renders inside that shell.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-ROUTING-AUDIT.md:195:**Confirmed live.** Classroom = warm cream editorial. StudentShell = white/black utility. Drill hub (inside the classroom shell) = black space imagery captioned "REASONING INSTRUMENT 01". Booking = burgundy. The drill hub is the sharpest break because it sits *inside* the classroom frame.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-ROUTING-AUDIT.md:234:**Traced.** `ProtectedRoute` returns children unconditionally on localhost in dev. Live consequence: the rail showed **Activities** padlocked while `/activities` rendered in full. That is correct in dev and invisible as a real problem, which is the danger — no permission regression can be caught before deploy.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-ROUTING-AUDIT.md:317:**C4 · L16 did not reproduce.** No `ref is not a prop` warning on `/classroom` or `/journal`. The cause is still in source — `ref={panelRef}` on a `motion.div` inside `AnimatePresence`, `StudentCornerBubble.tsx:216-218` — but that subtree only mounts when a live notification arrives, so the warning is conditional, not per-page-load. Keep it, restate it as conditional.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-ROUTING-AUDIT.md:530:Confirmed with data before fixing: 2 `guided_drill_assignments` rows, **1 distinct `guided_drill_id`**, assigned 6 minutes apart, both `assigned`. Guided rows now collapse by drill id. An open assignment beats a completed one — hiding still-owed work because a later twin was finished would be worse than showing a stale date — and the newest wins within that. Live: `/schedule` shows one drill row, not two.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-ROUTING-AUDIT.md:590:The root cause is bigger than `/booking`. `html` carries `dark`, but `--card` at the root still resolves to `0 0% 100%`, because **`.dark` in `index.css` is a deliberate alias that mirrors `.light`** — same values, `color-scheme: light`, commented "reserved alias". The genuinely dark shadcn tokens sit on `:root`, which both classes override. Every shadcn surface in the app is therefore light by design, and the classroom only looks dark because `classroom.css` and `home.css` draw their own `html.dark` rules in raw hex. `.booking-holo` then overrides the tokens again with its own deliberately-light burgundy theme.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\CLASSROOM-ROUTING-AUDIT.md:596:`--primary` is 68% lightness rather than the light theme\'s 27% because the accent is used as *text* on the card: at 58% it measured 4.13:1. Every text colour on the card now clears the floor, lowest 5.35:1, with translucent backgrounds composited before measuring.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\brainstorms\\2026-06-15-guided-drills-upgrade.md:6:- Filters: search by title, week dropdown, reasoning-type dropdown (hardcoded: Conditional Reasoning, Causation, Comparative Reasoning).\r\nprojects/lsatu\\brainstorms\\2026-06-15-guided-drills-upgrade.md:27:- **Canonical taxonomy:** `src/lib/questionFeatureFamilies.ts` — 60 distinct features grouped into families (Conditional & Formal Logic, Causal, Quantities/Sampling/Math, Argument Structure, Answer Choice Traps, Match/Similarity/Composition, ...). This is the source of truth for valid feature strings.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\brainstorms\\2026-06-15-guided-drills-upgrade.md:40:- Conditional & Formal Logic (848): Conditional Diagramming 222, Sufficient vs. Necessary Flaw 166, Conditional Conclusion 117, Contrapositive Logic 113, Logical Completion 82, Untriggered Conditional 67, Refute a Conditional 61, Complex Conditionals 20\r\nprojects/lsatu\\brainstorms\\2026-06-15-guided-drills-upgrade.md:91:- Use: backfill LSAT U\'s untagged questions (562 bank-wide; this covers the 101–109 subset). Note LSAT Lab\'s feature vocab is RICHER/different from LSAT U\'s 60 (108 distinct tags incl. topics like Science/Medical/Animals, reasoning like "Conditional Logic", and flaw subtypes) — will need mapping before importing into LSAT U.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:14:The second systemic problem is **performance at boot**: the main bundle is 3.7MB (1.03MB gz) because heavy pages (Drill at 3.5k lines, Auth, Intro to LR bootcamp) are eagerly imported, and QuestionBankProvider fires ~156 JSON fetches (~6MB) on every cold load for every route, even ones that never use questions.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:118:**Problem:** AssignmentRow calls `formatRelativeShort(assignment.assigned_at)` at line 379 but the file never imports or defines it. TS strict mode off plus no typecheck in the build means Vite ships it anyway; at runtime, rendering any assignment row with a non-null `assigned_at` throws `ReferenceError: formatRelativeShort is not defined`. Verified reachable: StudentOverview is rendered unconditionally by `HubWorkspace.tsx:65`, and AssignmentRow renders for each assignment from `useAdminTAAssignmentsForStudent`, so the hub crashes whenever a student has assignments with dates. Confirmed by `tsc` TS2304. The function exists at `src/lib/time.ts:17` and is imported correctly by ~10 other files (e.g. `HubAssignments.tsx:16`, `StudySidecar.tsx:63`).\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:152:### H8. Main entry bundle is 3.7MB (1.03MB gzipped) because heavy student surfaces are eagerly imported in App.tsx\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:239:**Problem:** In the set_role branch, `await admin.from(\'user_roles\').upsert(...)` (line 224) and the `.delete()` (line 228) never destructure or check `error`; the function unconditionally returns `{ success: true }` (line 234). If the mutation fails (constraint, RLS, transient), the admin UI shows the role changed when it did not. This directly violates the repo rule \'DB mutations never swallow errors\'. Note: verifyAdmin\'s role lookup (line 41) also ignores its error, but that fails closed, so it is only a UX issue.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:417:**Problem:** progress.ts documents \'resume where you left off\' and returns resolvedCount for exactly that, but ActivityRunner deliberately never resumes an in_progress run (comment at lines 71-74: Start begins fresh at question 1); resolvedCount is used by no caller (grep confirms only definition sites). Because ActivityItemView keeps `attempts` in local component state starting at 0 (ActivityItemView.tsx:52), a student who answered items in session 1 and reopens will re-record item 1 with attempt_no 1 again. The migration creating activity_item_responses has no UNIQUE constraint on (assignment_id, item_order, attempt_no), so the \'append-only replay log\' accumulates duplicate attempt_no rows; reconstructProgress sorts by attempt_no and treats rows[last] as the resolving attempt, so a prior session\'s cap-hitting attempt_no 3 can shadow a later correct retry (or vice versa), mis-scoring the teacher-facing ActivityResults view (ActivityResults.tsx:148) and the completed-run summary.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:424:**Problem:** data/drills.ts contains two entries named \'Value Assumption\' (ids value-assumption, value-assumption-2) and two named \'Sleazy Sales Pitch\' (sleazy-sales-pitch, sleazy-sales-pitch-2). Home.tsx builds stats per DRILLS entry keyed by drill.name against localStorage `lsat_flaw_performance` (which recordFlawPerformance keys by flaw name). Result: (a) the same saved stats render on two \'Practice Modules\' cards for each duplicated name; (b) `totalAttempts` at line 68 double-counts those flaws (\'Total Attempted: N Drills\' is inflated); (c) \'Method Mastery % Coverage\' at line 194 uses DRILLS.length (16) as the denominator while only 14 distinct flaw names can ever accumulate attempts, so 100% coverage is unreachable... actually reachable only because duplicates share stats, but the ratio is still computed over a padded denominator.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:564:**Problem:** JSON_FILES lists 155 section files (all present in public/data, 6.1 MB total). doLoad fetches all of them in parallel with `Promise.all(JSON_FILES.map(fetch))` on first QuestionBank use. That is a 155-request burst per uncached visitor (HTTP/2 helps but mobile/Lovable CDN cold starts still pay ~6 MB + per-request overhead), plus 155 JSON.parse calls and the full normalization pass on the main thread, blocking interaction on drill entry. There is also no persistence: every hard reload re-downloads and re-normalizes everything. The hand-maintained JSON_FILES list is itself a maintainability hotspot (currently in sync with disk, verified, but every ingest batch requires editing this 190-line array; a typo silently drops a section because fetch failures return null, see separate finding).\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:655:**Problem:** The stream loop does `const lines = chunk.split("\\n")` per chunk with no carry-over buffer. SSE frames routinely split mid-line across TCP chunks; a partial `data: {"choices":...` at a chunk boundary either fails the `line.startsWith("data:")` check or JSON.parse throws and is silently skipped (`catch { /* skip malformed SSE line */ }`). Result: missing words in the streamed tutor answer, and the loss is persisted because `accumulated` becomes the final message content.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:732:**Problem:** `if (totalQuestions === req.value)` awards a milestone only when overall_answered lands exactly on the threshold. Because updateDailyStats/overall counters can lose or batch increments (see race findings) or the caller may pass a stale userStats, crossing 100 questions at 101 means the \'100 questions\' badge is permanently unearnable. Also, every attempt runs one `user_achievements` existence query per candidate achievement inside the loop (N+1), plus the same pattern in checkStreakAchievements.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:800:### M147. Conditional React hooks in shape-landing-hero (rules-of-hooks violation)\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:805:**Fix plan:** Move both `useMotionValue` calls unconditionally to the top of the component body, then use the condition to select between `mousePosition?.x` and the fallback motion value when building the transforms. Verify with `npx eslint src/bootcamps/principle-flaw/components/ui/shape-landing-hero.tsx` and by rendering the Principle & Flaw hero in the dev server. Also consider deleting the unused `HeroGeometric` wrapper.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:875:**Problem:** On unique violation it SELECTs view_count then UPDATEs to view_count+1 as two separate requests. Two tabs (or a fast double-fire) lose increments, and if the SELECT returns null (row deleted between calls) it writes view_count = 2 unconditionally. Analytics-only impact.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT.md:947:**Fix plan:** Remove the is_admin conditionals from the JSX and memos: delete the NewConversationDialog block and its import, hardcode variant=\'student\' and the student emptyLabel, simplify activeHeaderName/studentPrimaryId to the student path. Also remove the now-unused StudentEmptyState/NewConversationDialog imports if orphaned.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\DESIGN.md:145:**The Workroom** is everything past sign-in. Light, neutral, and quiet, because a student sits inside it for an hour reading dense argument text and the interface must not compete with the passage. There is no warmth here and that is the decision, not an oversight: warm accents need a dark ground to read, and on the Workroom\'s white they collapse. The Hall\'s bronze measures 8.58:1 on its own night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom\'s page. The two registers do not share a palette because they cannot.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\DESIGN.md:196:**The Meaning-Only Rule.** A colour appears in the Workroom because something is right, wrong, cautionary, or dangerous. There is no fifth reason. If a surface feels flat and the impulse is to add a colour, the answer is type and space.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\DESIGN.md:246:This is a decision, not a description. The codebase currently ships five shadow tokens tuned for a dark ground, from `0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` up to `0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.7)`, and applies the smallest of them to every card. Because the app ships light by default, those shadows land at 40 percent black under a white card. The light theme never redefined them, and the one glow token is white, so it is invisible on white. The tokens are retired by this record and the code has not caught up yet.\r\nprojects/lsatu\\content\\lsat\\logical-reasoning\\logical-reasoning-guide.sidecar.json:799: It\'s easier to prove a weakly worded claim. But since most of the inferences on Must Be True come from Conditional or Mathematical thinking, they often are strongly worded, because we can often derive certainty using conditional / mathematical thinking.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:183:## 4. Conditional Logic (Easier) Set 1\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:188:title: Conditional Logic (Easier) Set 1\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:195:reasoning_type: Conditional Logic\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:196:question_feature: Conditional Logic\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:204:# Try 9 questions that feature conditional logic\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:208:These are the LR question types that most often would prompt us to consider diagramming the Conditional Logic:\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:218:- Look out for conditional trigger words.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:220:- Practice diagramming some of these problems, by writing conditionals on your paper. But also practice trusting your instinct _not_ to diagram the conditional logic, when you feel like you\'re having no trouble absorbing the ideas.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:222:How does Conditional Logic usually show up, by type?\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:224:- **Principle-Conform w/ Principle in Stimulus** (makes a lot of sense to diagram these conditionals, because all five answers are testing us on whether we have an argument that establishes the left side of a rule and concludes the right side of a rule)\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:230:- **Must Be True / Most Supported** (if there are multiple conditionals, they will often chain together. otherwise, a fact provided will trigger the contrapositive of a conditional)\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:232:- **Flaw / Parallel Flaw** (if we\'re doing Flaw and see conditional logic, it usually means that the author is about to use that conditional rule in an illegal Backwards or Opposite fashion, which is the #1 Famous Flaw, known as **Necessary vs. Sufficient**)\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:261:- conditional indicators (if, only if, unless, requires, etc.)\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:393:When they are supplying a **missing idea**, Necessary Assumption questions feel a lot like Sufficient Assumption or Principle-Strengthen. Sometimes they even feel like Must Be True, which makes sense because we\'re being asked what Must Be Assumed. These examples are heavy on conditional logic, and the correct answer seems to be regurgitating concepts that were mentioned in the stimulus. ("in-the-box" answers)\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:399:We don\'t, but generally speaking if we see conditional logic or see certain buzzwords / phrases being repeated twice within the paragraph, then we\'re being tested on a missing idea.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:427:if an answer is **conditional**, diagram it. see whether that conditional (or more likely its contrapositive) matches a reasoning move the author made. be wary of illegal reversals or negations of the author\'s thinking.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:450:  - Conditional Logic\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:463:## Conditional Logic\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:470:- valid and invalid conditional inferences\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:500:What idea goes on the **Left** of the conditional arrow?  \r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:502:When we\'re using **Conditional Logic** to "prove an argument", the **Left** side should be triggered by facts we know from the Evidence (or Answer Choice), and the **Right** side of the rule should match the Conclusion\'s language.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:601:In many cases, causal chains overlap with conditional logic. If we see conditional logic wording, like **"if, when, only, only if, unless, requires, always, will"**, then we can choose to think of it conditionally or causally, same difference.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:622:Yes, basically! It feels _naughty_ because that would be an illegal negation in the world of conditional logic, but in the world of Causality, you\'ll find LSAT being cool with the common sense plausibility of "if cause didn\'t happen, effect wouldn\'t have happened".\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:664:- Is there an _Untriggered Conditional_ idea in the evidence?\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:799:When the stimulus is providing an explicit principle (or two), we\'re usually being tested on our ability to apply a conditional rule.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:801:To find an answer that Conforms to this principle, we want an answer where the situation described matches the Trigger of the principle, and the judgment concluded matches the Outcome of the principle. (Even when the principles aren\'t expressed as conditionals, there will still usually be two things we need to make sure match up).\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:821:When we\'re dealing with Conditionals, then the trigger of the conditional should match the Evidence and the outcome of the conditional should match the Conclusion.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:838:question_feature: First Taste / Conditional Principle\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:848:All of these examples are the much-more common style of Principle-Strengthen in which the _answers_ are principles. They often feel more like **Sufficient Assumption** than Strengthen, because the correct answer is usually a conditional rule that is triggered by the Evidence and results in the Conclusion.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:852:Nevertheless, our primary approach to these is to expect a conditional logic answer, so we pre-phrase by articulating an "IF premise, THEN conclusion" reasoning move.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:854:Most of the answers will be conditionals. If so, we want to be very **stubborn with the outcome**. The right side of the rule should match the conclusion (or at least sound like wording that would strongly support the conclusion). We want to be **flexible with the trigger**.\r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:858:Given this argument, we\'d anticipate a conditional answer  \r\nprojects/lsatu/data/guided-drills\\week_2_blue_lr_guided_drills.md:876:- Try to get rid of a lot answers quickly because they don\'t have any good matching language for the conclusion, or because Do not hand-edit generated types.\r\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\r\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\r\n\r\n## Stack And Commands\r\n\r\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\r\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\r\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\r\n- Build: `npm run build`\r\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\r\n- Test: `npm run test`\r\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\r\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\r\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\r\n\r\n## Working Rules\r\n\r\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\r\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\r\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\r\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\r\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\r\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\r\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\r\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\r\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\r\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\r\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\r\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\r\n\r\n## Smart Drill Note\r\n\r\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\r\n\r\n## Verification (must not skip)\r\n\r\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\r\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\r\n\r\n## Workflow Expectations\r\n\r\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\r\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\r\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\r\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\r\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\r\n\r\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\r\n\r\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\r\n\r\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\r\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\r\n\r\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\r\n\r\n## Codex Adapter Layout\r\n\r\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\r\n\r\n# Guided Drills Admin Upgrade: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes\r\nDate: 2026-06-15 · Goal: Define what "upgrade and enhance" means for the admin-only Guided Drills catalog (`src/pages/admin/GuidedDrills.tsx`) so it can be implemented as small, safe changes.\r\n\r\n## Current state (from code inspection)\r\n- File: `src/pages/admin/GuidedDrills.tsx`. Renders a single flat `<table>` of all 108 drills across 11 weeks, no grouping/pagination.\r\n- Filters: search by title, week dropdown, reasoning-type dropdown (hardcoded: Conditional Reasoning, Causation, Comparative Reasoning).\r\n- Columns: Wk (W#.#), Title, Category, Q-Types (badges), Reasoning (badges), Feature, # (question_count), Pool (resolved live from bank_filter; turns amber when pool < needed).\r\n- Per-row actions: Open HTML (`/guided-drills/:slug`), Markdown Preview (sheet, left/right instruction card), Assign (sheet: optional schedule date + student checkboxes → createAssignments + classroom item + notify).\r\n- Data: `listGuidedDrills()` / `resolveGuidedDrillPool()` from `@/lib/guidedDrills`; students from `useHubStudents`.\r\n\r\n## Summary / key decisions\r\n- **Real goal is NOT layout/visual.** It\'s data correctness: every guided drill must have the right *question features* assigned and be correctly configured to pull the *right* questions from the question database. This is a config/audit problem, not a redesign.\r\n\r\n## Q&A log\r\n### Q1 — What\'s actually broken\r\n- Asked: Is the pain findability, visual quality, missing capability, or broken behavior?\r\n- Captured: None of the layout framings. Preferred; deterministic.\r\n  2. **Dynamic filter** — `bank_filter` = { qtypes[], features[], reasoning_types[], difficulty_min/max, count }. Falls back to this when no exact qids.\r\n- Pool match logic: qtype (exact, lowercased) ∩ features (ANY match) ∩ reasoning_types (ANY match) ∩ difficulty range. So `bank_filter.features` strings MUST exactly match the bank\'s `questionFeatures` vocabulary (lowercased compare) or the drill silently pulls the wrong/empty pool.\r\n- Separate display field `question_features` (shown in the "Feature" column) is NOT the same as `bank_filter.features` (the selector). They can drift.\r\n- **Canonical taxonomy:** `src/lib/questionFeatureFamilies.ts` — 60 distinct features grouped into families (Conditional & Formal Logic, Causal, Quantities/Sampling/Math, Argument Structure, Answer Choice Traps, Match/Similarity/Composition, ...). LSAT Lab has its OWN feature taxonomy (may differ from LSAT U\'s 60).\r\n- Show-entries combobox set to 100 → full PT fits one page. Selection-mode checkboxes (seen in Joshua\'s screenshot) appear in that flow.\r\n- Extraction plan: per PT 101–109, set PT filter, get_page_text, parse rows → CSV (TEST,S,Q,NAME,DIFFICULTY,ATTEMPTS [+ feature/type if we capture per-filter]).\r\n\r\n## EXECUTION: per-PT set creation (decided: one set per PT, create directly)\r\n- Working flow (verified on PT101):\r\n  1. Success modal → "Return to Page" resets builder (selection cleared, PT filter persists).\r\n- Naming convention: "PT 1XX LR".\r\n- DONE: **PT 101 LR** (51 questions) ✅ created 2026-06-15.\r\n- PIVOT: Joshua built his own combined set; per-PT creation abandoned after PT101.\r\n\r\n## INGESTION RESULT (the real deliverable)\r\n- Discovery: the bank table\'s DataTables row objects already hold each question\'s full metadata client-side. The `question.subtypes` array = LSAT Lab\'s Question Features (+ subtype + topics).
